# Ordinary two-point correlations of multiplicative functions

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-AL-H-0021` | `PM-007.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Ordinary-two-point-correlations-of-multiplicative-functions-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Ordinary-two-point-correlations-of-multiplicative-functions-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the ordinary two-point Chowla conjecture. For every fixed pair of nonproportional affine forms, the Liouville correlation has a power-of-logarithm saving at every cutoff, with an absolute exponent. We also prove the binary corrected Elliott conjecture for ordinary averages of complex multiplicative functions of modulus at most one, under uniform nonpretentiousness of at least one original factor. This qualitative conclusion holds in fixed residue classes and for fixed nonproportional affine forms.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the ordinary two-point Chowla conjecture. For every fixed pair of nonproportional affine forms, the Liouville correlation has a power-of-logarithm saving at every cutoff, with an absolute exponent. We also prove the binary corrected Elliott conjecture for ordinary averages of complex multiplicative functions of modulus at most one, under uniform nonpretentiousness of at least one original factor. This qualitative conclusion holds in fixed residue classes and for fixed nonproportional affine forms.

- **claim-affine-liouville-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-q-affine)): There is an absolute constant $c>0$ such that, for every fixed choice of integers $a_1,a_2\ge1$ and $b_1,b_2\ge0$ with $a_1b_2-a_2b_1\ne0$, there is a constant $C_{a_1,a_2,b_1,b_2}$ for which $$\left\|\sum_{1\le n\le X} \lambda(a_1n+b_1)\lambda(a_2n+b_2)\right\| \le C_{a_1,a_2,b_1,b_2}\frac{X}{(\log X)^c} \qquad(X\ge3).$$ The cutoff $X$ may be any real number. No coprimality condition is imposed on the coefficients.
  - Explanation: Ordinary affine Liouville correlations have a logarithmic saving with an absolute exponent; the multiplier depends on the fixed forms.
- **claim-binary-corrected-elliott** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-elliott)): Let $f_1,f_2:\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ be multiplicative, and suppose that at least one satisfies <a href="#eq:unp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:unp">[eq:unp]</a>. For every fixed pair of distinct nonnegative integers $h_1,h_2$, $$\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_1(n+h_1)f_2(n+h_2)\longrightarrow0 \qquad(N\longrightarrow\infty,\ N\in\mathbb N).$$
  - Explanation: Ordinary shifted products of bounded multiplicative functions tend to zero when an original factor satisfies uniform nonpretentiousness.
- **claim-affine-general-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-affine)): Let $f_1,f_2:\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ be multiplicative, with at least one satisfying <a href="#eq:unp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:unp">[eq:unp]</a>. For all fixed integers $a_1,a_2\ge1$ and $b_1,b_2\ge0$ with $a_1b_2-a_2b_1\ne0$, $$\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_1(a_1n+b_1)f_2(a_2n+b_2) \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The qualitative cancellation extends to fixed nonproportional affine forms through finite local expansions.
- **claim-liouville-every-progression** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-progression)): There is one absolute $c>0$ such that, for every fixed $h,l\ge1$ and $b\in\mathbb Z$, $$F(X):=\frac1X\sum_{1\le n\le X} \mathbf 1_{n\equiv b\pmod l}\lambda(n)\lambda(n+h) \ll_{h,l}(\log X)^{-c}\qquad(X\ge3).$$ The estimate holds for every real $X$, with no condition on $\gcd(b,l)$.
  - Explanation: The same absolute logarithmic exponent controls every fixed progression, including nonunit classes, at every real cutoff.
- **claim-retained-bin-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-bin-mass)): For sufficiently large $X$, $$\frac12SV_*\le S_0\le SV_*.$$
  - Explanation: The bin restrictions retain a positive fraction of the full harmonic divisor mass.
- **claim-finite-residue-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-finite-law)): There is an absolute constant $C_1$ with the following property. Choose $A>C_1+20$. Let $l\ge1$ be fixed, and let $\mathcal R$ be any set of primes at most $e^L$, none dividing $l$. On the coordinates modulo $p\in\mathcal R$ and modulo $l$, let $\mu$ be the product of the uniform laws; the modulus-one coordinate can be omitted. Suppose that an event $\mathcal E$ is represented by a circuit of depth at most $20$ and size at most $\exp(L^6)$ in these residue equalities. For every consecutive integer interval $I$ with $\|I\|\ge\exp(L^A/2)$, $$\label{q:finite-law-bound} \bigl\|\mathbb E_I\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E}-\mu(\mathcal E)\bigr\| \le e^{-L^{10}}$$ for sufficiently large $L$ depending on $l$. The bound is uniform in the position of $I$ and in all offsets used by the tests. The modulus $l$ need not be squarefree.
  - Explanation: Bounded residue circuits have nearly the same acceptance probability under long integer intervals and independent uniform residue coordinates.
- **claim-scalar-residue-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-finite-expansion)): Under the hypotheses of [q:finite-law](#q:finite-law), write $\mathbb E$ for expectation under $\mu$. Suppose $\Phi=\sum_\alpha c_\alpha\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_\alpha}$, where every event has the stated circuit bounds and $\sum_\alpha\|c_\alpha\|\le\exp(C L^5)$ for a fixed constant $C$. Then, for sufficiently large $L$, $$\label{q:scalar-comparison} \|\mathbb E_I\Phi-\mathbb E\Phi\|\le e^{-L^9}.$$ This also applies to the sum of errors for a family of such expansions when the same bound holds for their total absolute coefficient sum.
  - Explanation: Controlled absolute coefficient mass allows the residue comparison to be summed across scalar expansions without discarding signs before expectation.
- **claim-truncated-weight-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-truncated-weight)): For every offset $a$ and every interval $I$ allowed in [q:finite-law](#q:finite-law), $$\label{q:truncated-weight-average} \mathbb E_I\bigl[w(n+a)\mathbf 1_{\omega_Q(n+a)\le400\log L}\bigr] \le S+e^{-L^9}\le2S$$ for sufficiently large $L$. The analogous sum over $M$ fixed offsets is at most $2MS$.
  - Explanation: Exact low-degree padding states bound the integer average of the truncated vertex weight by its independent-residue mean.
- **claim-quantitative-centering** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-center)): With the parameters and prime supplies fixed above, for every sufficiently large real $X$ one has $$\label{q:center-estimate} \left\|\sum_j C_j-S_0F(X)\right\| \ll_{h,l}SV_*\left( \eta+X^{-1}+\eta^{-1}2^JL^{-1/20}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Rough-shift cancellation bounds the aggregate discrepancy between the centered bin sums and their full-divisibility main term.
- **claim-rough-fourier-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-rough-fourier)): Let $L$ be sufficiently large, let $h\ge1$ be an integer, and suppose $$\tfrac12\exp(L^{0.995})\le D\le\exp(2L).$$ Let $\mathcal Z\subset[D,2D)$ consist of integers with no prime factor at most $\exp(L^{0.99})$. For $$B(\theta)=\sum_{z\in\mathcal Z}\frac{\mathrm e(hz\theta)}z \qquad(\theta\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)$$ one has, with absolute implied constants, $$\label{q:rough-bounds} \\|B\\|_\infty\ll L^{-0.99},\qquad \int_0^1\|B(\theta)\|^4\,d\theta\ll D^{-1}L^{-3.96}.$$
  - Explanation: Rough support bounds both the maximum and the fourth moment of the divisor exponential sum.
- **claim-liouville-short-exponential-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-mrt-short)): For every pair of real numbers $10\le H\le Z$, $$\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb R} \int_0^Z \left\|\sum_{x\le n\le x+H}\lambda(n)\mathrm e(\alpha n)\right\|\,dx \ll HZ\left(\frac{\log\log H}{\log H} +(\log Z)^{-1/700}\right),$$ with an absolute implied constant. The supremum is outside the integral.
  - Explanation: The cited short-interval estimate controls the average absolute exponential sum with the frequency supremum outside the integral.
- **claim-rough-shift-correlation** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-rough-shifts)): Fix integers $h,l\ge1$ and $b_0\in\mathbb Z$. Let $A\ge1000$, let $L$ be sufficiently large, and suppose $Y\ge\exp(L^A/2)$. Let $D$ be a positive integer and let $\mathcal Z\subset[D,2D)$ satisfy the assumptions of Lemma <a href="#q:rough-fourier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="q:rough-fourier">10</a>. Then $$\label{q:rough-correlation} \left\|\frac1Y\sum_{1\le v\le Y} \sum_{z\in\mathcal Z}\frac1z \mathbf 1_{v\equiv b_0\pmod l}\lambda(v)\lambda(v+hz)\right\| \ll_{h,l}L^{-21/20}.$$
  - Explanation: The rough Fourier bounds and the short-interval input yield cancellation averaged over rough shifts in any fixed residue class.
- **claim-padding-anticoncentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-padding)): For every $d\in\mathcal D$ and the bin width $\eta=e^{-J}$, $$\label{q:padding-square} \mathbb E^*\sum_j\rho_j(n,d)^2\ll_{h,l}L^{-1}.$$ The constant is independent of $d$, $W$, and the location of the bins.
  - Explanation: Under the tilted site law, padding mass cannot concentrate strongly in the logarithmic bins, uniformly in the tuple label.
- **claim-deletion-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-cuts)): Suppose the family $\mathcal B_j$ satisfies the covariance, probability, and circuit hypotheses just stated. Then $$\label{q:cuts-bound} \frac1{SV_*}\sum_j\|C_j-C_j^\circ\| \ll_{h,l} 2^J\bigl(K^{-1}+L^{-100}+e^{-2WJ}\bigr) +\exp(-L^{0.9}).$$ This estimate holds for the integer averages defining $C_j$ and $C_j^\circ$, for every sufficiently large real $X$.
  - Explanation: Degree, padding concentration and rare-site cuts have a small total normalized cost after transfer to integer averages.
- **claim-reciprocal-count-elimination** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-crude-count)): Consider at most $R\le4L$ signed steps together with additional records having at most $L^{O(1)}$ choices per prime slot. Then the number of equality patterns and such records is at most $\exp(O(R\log^2L))$. With one reciprocal for every distinct prime, the sum over their numerical values has the same bound, also after multiplication by $Lu(q_i)$ for each step.* *Suppose, with the padding values fixed, that a recorded relation in the tuple primes is $$az+c\equiv0\pmod p,\qquad a\not\equiv0\pmod p,$$ where $a,c,p$ are independent of the selected prime variable $z$. Its reciprocal sum costs at most $(1+L)/H_0$ in place of an unrestricted prime sum. Such savings multiply when selected variables are ordered so that each relation uses only its own variable, earlier selected variables, and nonselected variables.
  - Explanation: A coarse equality-pattern count is paired with reciprocal savings from invertible congruences eliminated in reverse dependency order.
- **claim-prohibited-event-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-bad)): The event in [q:prohibited](#q:prohibited) is translation covariant and satisfies $$\mathbb P(n\in\mathcal B_j) \le\exp\bigl(-\tfrac12L^{1-\delta}\bigr).$$ As a function of the residue equalities it has a Boolean circuit of depth two and size at most $\exp(L^3)$ for sufficiently large $L$.
  - Explanation: The witness-start event is rare, translation covariant and representable by a shallow residue circuit.
- **claim-nonbacktracking-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-trace)): There is an absolute constant $C_2$ such that $$\label{q:trace-bound} \mathbb E\\|H^k\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le\bigl[K(C_2\sqrt W)^J\bigr]^{2k}.$$ The norm is the unnormalized Hilbert–Schmidt norm. The same estimate, after increasing $C_2$ absolutely, holds when block origins are averaged over any integer interval of length at least $\exp(\tfrac12L^A)$. The threshold for $L$ may depend on the fixed data.
  - Explanation: The nonbacktracking moment has only square-root dependence on the reciprocal mass of each centered prime supply.
- **claim-singleton-mixed-difference** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-mixed-difference)): The absolute expectation of a designated term of <a href="#q:word-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="q:word-product">[q:word-product]</a> is at most $$\label{q:mixed-majorant} \mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lit\ consistency}}\,b_{\mathcal L} \mathbb E\bigl[R\|\Delta G\|\bigr].$$ In particular $\|\Delta G\|\le2^{S_1}$.
  - Explanation: Exact integration converts singleton centering into a mixed difference of deletion predicates before absolute values are taken.
- **claim-many-unlit-negligible** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-many-unlit)): The total contribution to the moment majorant from designated terms with $U>L^{1/50}$ is at most $\exp(-L^{1+\delta})$.
  - Explanation: Many unlit repeated occurrences supply enough extra reciprocal powers to make their total contribution negligible.
- **claim-random-prime-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-rank-probability)): Let $\mathscr P$ be a finite nonempty set of primes, and let $\mu$ be a probability measure on $\mathscr P$ with $\max_{p\in\mathscr P}\mu(p)\le\alpha$. Let $r\ge1$, let $B\in M_r(\mathbb Z)$ be nonsingular over $\mathbb R$, and let $f:\mathscr P^r\to\mathbb Z^r$ be any function. Suppose that $M\ge2$ and $$\left\|(B(y-y'))_i\right\|\le M \quad\text{for all }y,y'\in\mathscr P^r\text{ and }1\le i\le r.$$ If the $2r$ coordinates of $c,y\in\mathscr P^r$ are independent with law $\mu$, then $$\label{q:rank-probability-bound} \mathbb P\bigl(c_i\mid(By+f(c))_i\text{ for every }i\bigr) \le \left[\alpha(1+d_M)\right]^{r/2}, \qquad d_M=\left\lfloor\frac{\log M}{\log2}\right\rfloor.$$
  - Explanation: Decoupling cancels the arbitrary control-dependent vector, and real matrix rank bounds the probability of zero coordinates without modular invertibility.
- **claim-high-rank-words-negligible** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-rank)): In the sum of the nonnegative majorants from [q:mixed-difference](#q:mixed-difference), the total contribution of words having a column with $r$ jointly independent pairs <a href="#q:rank-pair" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="q:rank-pair">[q:rank-pair]</a> is $$O\left(\exp(-c_0 L^{203/200})\right)$$ for some absolute $c_0>0$ and sufficiently large $L$. The bound includes the external dimension factors in the trace expansion and is uniform in the bin. The threshold may depend on the fixed parameters $W,h,l$.
  - Explanation: A column with sufficiently many jointly independent lit-consistency pairs has negligible total reciprocal majorant.
- **claim-common-hybrid-witness-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-q-witness-cover)): Fix a numerical main word with singleton set $\mathcal U$, where $S_1=\|\mathcal U\|>L^{1/4}$, and fix a draw of the residue coordinates. If $\Delta G\ne0$, then some hybrid contains $T$ positive witnesses attached at main departures, each of length at most $s$, with distinct marked primes $y_1,\ldots,y_T\in\mathcal U$. The prime $y_j$ occurs in witness $j$ and in none of the other $T-1$ witnesses. The combined length of the main word and these witnesses is at most $4L$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero singleton mixed difference forces a common hybrid containing witnesses with private marked singleton primes.
- **claim-minimal-witness-active-labels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-q-active)): Let $E_1,\ldots,E_m$ be a minimal prohibited word. For every $P$ label $y$ in this word, either $y$ is active or there are an active label $z$ and a step $i$ containing $z$ such that $$\label{q:active-prefix} \sum_{\substack{a<i\\y\mid E_a}}E_a\not\equiv0\pmod z.$$ The relations establishing activity have the form <a href="#q:active-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="q:active-definition">[q:active-definition]</a> with contiguous intervals.
  - Explanation: Each label has either an intrinsic invertible relation or a nonzero prefix contribution modulo an active label.
- **claim-singleton-class-negligible** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-singletons)): For a fixed bin, the total contribution to the absolute majorants in [q:mixed-difference](#q:mixed-difference) from main words with $S_1>L^{1/4}$, including the external factor $\|\mathcal D\|^2M$ in the trace expansion, is at most $$\exp(-L^{21/20})$$ for sufficiently large $L$, with the fixed parameters held fixed.
  - Explanation: Private witnesses provide successively usable arithmetic constraints that suppress words with many singleton labels.
- **claim-perfect-block-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-q-block-intervals)): In each such block, the occurrences of every column prime form an interval of consecutive positions. No nonempty subinterval of the block has total displacement zero.
  - Explanation: Survival of prohibited-path deletion forces interval-shaped prime occurrences and forbids zero-displacement subintervals inside perfect blocks.
- **claim-universal-forest-code** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-forest-code)): Suppose that $$S_1\le L^{1/4},\qquad U\le L^{1/50},\qquad r=\lfloor L^{1/50}\rfloor,$$ and that, in every column, there are no $r$ pairs <a href="#q:rank-pair" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="q:rank-pair">[q:rank-pair]</a> whose $2r$ vectors are jointly independent. Consider lit-consistent terms of [q:mixed-difference](#q:mixed-difference) with a nonzero integrand. The equality patterns that can occur in any one column belong to a set of cardinality at most $\exp(Ck)$, for an absolute constant $C$. This set can be chosen independently of all numerical coefficients and padding choices. Consequently there are at most $\exp(CJk)$ combined column patterns.
  - Explanation: A universal forest code bounds the union of possible column equality patterns over all numerical coefficients and padding choices.
- **claim-padding-path-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-padding-sum)): Fix an integer $m\ge1$, a bin $j$, tuples $d_1,\ldots,d_m\in\mathcal D$, signs $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_m\in\{-1,1\}$, an initial site, and the entire non-$P$ residue environment. With successive sites defined by $n_{i+1}=n_i+\varepsilon_i hq_i d_i$, one has $$\label{q:padding-product} \sum_{q_1,\ldots,q_m\in\mathcal Q} \prod_{i=1}^m \frac{Lu(q_i)}{w(n_i)}\mathbf 1_{q_i\mid n_i} \mathbf 1_{(d_i,q_i)\in\mathcal S_j} \mathbf 1_{\rho_j(n_i,d_i)\le K/L} \le K^m.$$ The bound is uniform over the fixed data and initial site.
  - Explanation: Backward induction bounds all padding choices while keeping one shared translated residue environment.
- **claim-noncommuting-spectral-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-noncommuting)): Let $N\ge1$ and let $B_1,\ldots,B_N$ be self-adjoint operators on a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space $\mathcal H$, and let $P$ be an orthogonal projection on $\mathcal H$. Define an operator $\mathscr B$ on $\mathcal H^N$ by $$\mathscr B_{i,j}=\mathbf 1_{i\ne j}B_j.$$ Suppose $a,b\ge0$, $\\|B_i\\|\le a$ for every $i$, and $$\sum_{i=1}^N\\|B_i v\\|^2\le b^2\\|v\\|^2 \qquad(v=Pv).$$ Writing $\rho(\mathscr B)$ for its spectral radius, one has $$\label{q:noncommuting-bound} \left\\|P\left(\sum_{i=1}^N B_i\right)P\right\\| \le 3\max\{a,b,\rho(\mathscr B)\}.$$
  - Explanation: Resolvent positivity transfers the nonbacktracking spectral radius to a projected sum without commuting distinct edge operators.
- **claim-weighted-row-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-weighted-rows)): On every block, in either the product model or the integer model, $$\begin{aligned} \\|A_d\\|&\le2K,\label{q:single-operator}\\ \sum_{d\in\mathcal D}\\|A_d v\\|^2 &\le4K^2(8W)^J\\|v\\|^2\qquad(v=Ev). \label{q:square-operators} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Weighted Schur bounds and the retained degree constraint control individual edge operators and their summed energy.
- **claim-most-blocks-compressed-norm** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-spectral)): There is an absolute constant $C_3$ with the following property. For each bin $j$, average block origins over any integer interval to which [q:trace](#q:trace) applies. Outside a fraction at most $e^{-2k}$ of these origins, $$\label{q:compressed-norm} \left\\|E\left(\sum_d A_d\right)E\right\\| \le K(C_3\sqrt W)^J.$$
  - Explanation: The moment estimate and spectral transfer give a compressed operator bound outside a small fraction of block origins.
- **claim-retained-bin-testing** (bound, [source](#stmt-q-testing)): For every bin $j$ and all sufficiently large $X$, $$\label{q:bin-estimate} \|C_j^\circ\|\ll \frac{S K(C_3\sqrt W)^J}{L}+e^{-L}.$$ The threshold may depend on the fixed $h,l,W$, while $C_3$ is the absolute constant in <a href="#q:C3-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="q:C3-choice">[q:C3-choice]</a>.
  - Explanation: Testing against truncated weighted Liouville vectors and counting overlapping edges bounds each retained bin correlation.
- **claim-qualitative-divisor-graph** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-graph-estimate)): Fix $h\ge1$, $1<\tau<2$, $C_0\ge1$, and $T>0$, and use <a href="#eq:graph-constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-constants">[eq:graph-constants]</a>–<a href="#eq:graph-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-weights">[eq:graph-weights]</a>. For every sufficiently large $B$, every admissible family $\mathcal D$, all permitted cutoffs $w_d$, all coefficients $\|a_d\|\le1$, and all functions $F,G:\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$, $$\label{eq:graph-estimate} \limsup_{X\to\infty}\frac1X \left\|\sum_{1\le x\le X}\sum_{d\in\mathcal D} a_dF(x)G(x+hd)g_d(x,x+hd)\right\| \ll L_0 B^{-1-c_*/2}.$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $B$ may depend on $h,\tau,C_0,T$, and the fixed constants in <a href="#eq:graph-constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-constants">[eq:graph-constants]</a>, but not on $H,\mathcal D,w_d,a_d,F,G$. All graph parameters are fixed when $X$ tends to infinity.
  - Explanation: The normalized divisor graph bounds bilinear sums against arbitrary bounded sequences with all graph parameters fixed in the long limit.
- **claim-divergent-absolute-defect** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-analytic-absolute-defect)): Let $f\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be multiplicative and $\|f\|\le1$. If $$\sum_p\frac{1-\|f(p)\|}{p}=\infty,$$ then $N^{-1}\sum_{n\le N}\|f(n)\|\to0$.
  - Explanation: Divergence of the reciprocal prime modulus defect forces the ordinary mean absolute value to vanish.
- **claim-positive-mass-divisor-bin** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-raw-bin)): Let $f_1,f_2\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be $1$-bounded multiplicative functions satisfying <a href="#eq:analytic-finite-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-finite-defects">[eq:analytic-finite-defects]</a> and $f_1(1)=f_2(1)=1$. Fix $1<\tau<2$. There are constants $C_0\ge1$ and $c>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $B$, one can choose $1\le H\le\exp(C_0B)$ and a family $\mathcal D$ of squarefree $\mathcal S$-products satisfying $$\begin{gathered} H<d\le\tau H,\qquad d>1,\qquad \omega(d)\le J=\lceil C_0\log B\rceil, \qquad \|f_1(d)f_2(d)\|\ge\tfrac12, \label{eq:raw-selected-divisors}\\ \sum_{d\in\mathcal D}\frac{A^{\omega_C(d)}}d \ge c\frac{L_0}{B}. \label{eq:raw-bin-mass} \end{gathered}$$ The constants are independent of $B$ and of the long averaging variable.
  - Explanation: A finite divisor probability law and pigeonholing provide an admissible interval with positive reciprocal weight and nonvanishing factor values.
- **claim-biased-raw-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-raw-lower-bound)): Let $f_1,f_2\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be $1$-bounded multiplicative functions, with $f_1(1)=f_2(1)=1$, satisfying <a href="#eq:analytic-bias" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-bias">[eq:analytic-bias]</a> and <a href="#eq:analytic-finite-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-finite-defects">[eq:analytic-finite-defects]</a>. Choose $1<\tau<2$ and $T>0$ with $\tau-1\le\gamma/64$ and $32/T^2\le\gamma/64$, and use a cutoff $\phi$ as in <a href="#eq:analytic-cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-cutoffs">[eq:analytic-cutoffs]</a>, equal to $1$ on $[-T/2,T/2]$. With $\mathcal D,H$ supplied by [lem:raw-bin](#lem:raw-bin), set $$a_d=\overline{f_1(d)f_2(d)},\qquad X_j=HN_j.$$ There is $c_\gamma>0$, independent of $B,j$, such that, for every sufficiently large fixed $B$, $$\label{eq:raw-lower-bound} \liminf_{j\to\infty}\frac{\|R_B(X_j)\|}{X_j} \ge c_\gamma\frac{L_0}{B}.$$
  - Explanation: A persistent complex correlation bias yields a raw divisor sum large enough to contradict the centered graph estimate.
- **claim-general-analytic-centering** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-analytic-centering)): Let $f_1,f_2\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be multiplicative, with $\|f_i\|\le1$, and suppose at least one is uniformly nonpretentious. Fix $h\ge1$, $1<\tau<2$, $C_0\ge1$, $T>0$, and a smooth function $0\le\phi\le1$ supported in $[-T,T]$. For each sufficiently large $B$, let $\mathcal D,H,J$ satisfy <a href="#eq:divisor-family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:divisor-family">[eq:divisor-family]</a>, let $\|a_d\|\le1$, and use <a href="#eq:analytic-cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-cutoffs">[eq:analytic-cutoffs]</a>. Define $R_B,C_B$ by <a href="#eq:raw-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:raw-sum">[eq:raw-sum]</a> and <a href="#eq:analytic-centered-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-centered-sum">[eq:analytic-centered-sum]</a>. Then $$\label{eq:analytic-centering-bound} \limsup_{X\to\infty}\frac{\|R_B(X)-C_B(X)\|}{X} \ll L_0\left(B^{-1-\varepsilon}+\frac{J}{P_0}\right).$$ The implied constant may depend on the fixed parameters and $\phi$, but is independent of the divisor family and coefficients. The long-variable limit is taken with $B$ fixed.
  - Explanation: Rough Fourier multipliers and uniform short-interval cancellation make centering cheaper than the raw bias scale.
- **claim-finite-prime-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-prime-stability)): Let $f,b\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be multiplicative and $1$-bounded. Suppose their prime values agree outside a finite set $\mathcal P$. For every Dirichlet character $\chi$, every real $t$, and $X\ge2$, $$\label{eq:analytic-distance-stability} \left\|D(b,\chi n^{it};X)^2-D(f,\chi n^{it};X)^2\right\| \le2\sum_{p\in\mathcal P}\frac1p.$$ Consequently, if $f$ is uniformly nonpretentious, the same divergence holds uniformly over all such $b$ and over every fixed finite family of Dirichlet characters.
  - Explanation: Finite changes of prime values perturb squared pretentious distance by a bounded amount and preserve uniform divergence.
- **claim-general-short-exponential-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-mrt)): Let $X\ge D\ge10$ and let $b\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ be a $1$-bounded multiplicative function. With $Q=\min((\log X)^{1/125},(\log D)^5)$, one has $$\label{eq:mrt-input} \begin{split} \sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z}\int_0^X \left\|\sum_{y<m\le y+D}b(m)\mathrm e(\alpha m)\right\|\,dy \ll DX\left( e^{-\mathcal M(b;X,Q)/20} +\frac{\log\log D}{\log D} +\frac1{(\log X)^{1/700}}\right). \end{split}$$ The implied constant is absolute.
  - Explanation: The cited corrected short-interval theorem bounds exponential sums through the nonpretentiousness distance and interval-length errors.
- **claim-general-rough-multiplier** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rough-multiplier)): Fix $C_0\ge1$, $1<\tau<2$, and an integer $h\ge1$. Let $P_0=\exp(B^{1-\varepsilon})$, where $\varepsilon=10^{-4}$, and suppose $P_0/\tau\le M\le\exp(C_0B)$. If $\|c_w\|\le1$ and $c_w=0$ unless $w\in(M,\tau M]$ is $P_0$-rough, then, for all sufficiently large $B$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|Q_M\\|_\infty&\ll B^{-1+\varepsilon}\log B, \label{eq:rough-sup}\\ \int_{\mathbb R/\mathbb Z}\|Q_M(\alpha)\|^4\,d\alpha &\ll M^{-1}B^{-4+4\varepsilon}(\log B)^6. \label{eq:rough-fourth} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in $M$ and the coefficients.
  - Explanation: A finite sieve and Fourier orthogonality control arbitrary coefficients supported on rough integers in a short multiplicative interval.
- **claim-primitive-path-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-descent)): If a specification qualifies, a primitive specification using only its prime support qualifies at one of its path vertices, along a contiguous subpath in one of the two orientations.
  - Explanation: Strictly decreasing path length produces a qualifying primitive contiguous subpath using only the original prime support.
- **claim-deleted-set-high-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-high-trace)): For the divisor family, cutoffs, and kernels of [sec:graph-setup](#sec:graph-setup), the periodic set $Y$ in [def:primitive-specification](#def:primitive-specification) satisfies, as $B\to\infty$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P(n\notin Y)&\le P_0^{-1+o(1)},\label{eq:deleted-density}\\ \sum_{\boldsymbol d} \left\|\mathbb EK_{\boldsymbol d}(n) \prod_{j=0}^{\ell}\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\right\| &\le B^{-(1+c_*)\ell}.\label{eq:high-trace} \end{aligned}$$ The sum is over all closed lines of length $\ell$. The asymptotic bounds are uniform over the admissible divisor families and cutoffs, with $h,\tau,C_0,T$ fixed.
  - Explanation: The retained periodic vertex set has small deleted density and a strong signed closed-line trace bound.
- **claim-primitive-deletion-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-density)): The set $Y$ satisfies <a href="#eq:deleted-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deleted-density">[eq:deleted-density]</a>.
  - Explanation: Summing the extra prime first and then coarse reciprocal patterns bounds the deleted-set density.
- **claim-primitive-prime-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-properties)): For sufficiently large $B$, let a primitive specification have edge labels $e_1,\ldots,e_m$, extra prime $p$, and suffix ending at $m$. Then the following statements hold.* 1. *For every edge prime $q$, the indices $i$ with $q\mid e_i$ form an interval of consecutive indices.* 2. *Let $b$ be the first index of the final maximal constant block $e_b=\cdots=e_m$. Then $b>1$, the signs on this block are constant, and there exists $r\mid e_b$ with $r\nmid e_{b-1}$. Every such $r$ occurs only in the constant tail. In the specified suffix displacement, its coefficient as a prime variable is nonzero modulo $p$. This coefficient assertion holds for any edge prime whose occurrences are confined to that tail.* 3. *If $q$ occurs before $b$ and $r$ is as in the preceding part, the coefficient of $q$ in $z_{b-1}-z_0$ is nonzero modulo $r$.* *Coefficients here are obtained by fixing all other distinct prime values; squarefreeness makes each displacement linear in the prime being varied.
  - Explanation: Primitivity forces interval occurrences and supplies invertible coefficients from the final constant block and the preceding prefix.
- **claim-absolute-slot-majorant** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-crude-count)): Consider a line of length $\ell$ together with at most $2t_0$ attached qualifying specifications, each of length at most $L$. Refine its slot pattern by recording the lit or unlit status of every line occurrence. There are $\exp(O(B\log^2B))$ slot patterns, including signs, attachments, occurrence statuses, and recorded vertex identifications. For each assigned pattern and its numerical prime values, let $\mathfrak A$ be its attached specifications and let $E$ be any residue event imposing the recorded occurrence statuses, possibly with further restrictions. Then $$\label{eq:absolute-pattern-majorant} \mathbb E\left[\|K_{\boldsymbol d}(n)\|\mathbf 1_E(n) \prod_{\sigma\in\mathfrak A} \mathbf 1_{\{\sigma\text{ qualifies at its assigned vertex}\}}\right] \le A^{\ell J}\prod_{p\text{ used}}\frac1p.$$ If the recorded statuses include at least $t_0$ unlit occurrences among center primes occurring at least twice on the line, this majorant has the additional factor $P_0^{-t_0/2}$. The total contribution of that class, summed over all patterns and numerical assignments, is $O(\exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}))$.
  - Explanation: A coarse slot enumeration supplies one reciprocal per used prime, with extra savings from repeated unlit center occurrences.
- **claim-triangular-arithmetic-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-triangular-saving)): In the positive count of [lem:crude-count](#lem:crude-count), suppose every configuration under consideration supplies at least $B^{2\varepsilon}$ distinct prime variables $q_1,\ldots,q_k$ and tests with the following properties. Test $j$ uses only $q_j$, the earlier variables $q_1,\ldots,q_{j-1}$, and nonselected variables, and is either* 1. *$q_j\mid D_j$, where $D_j\ne0$ is independent of $q_j$ and $\log(\|D_j\|+2)=O(B\log B)$; or* 2. *$a_jq_j+c_j\equiv0\pmod{r_j}$, where $a_j,c_j,r_j$ are independent of $q_j$, $r_j$ is an earlier or nonselected prime variable, and $a_j\not\equiv0\pmod{r_j}$.* *Assume the choices of tests and their order have $\exp(O(B\log^2B))$ descriptions. Then their total absolute contribution, also allowing the attached specifications in [lem:crude-count](#lem:crude-count), is $$\label{eq:negligible-constraints} O\bigl(\exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2})\bigr).$$ The description bound applies in particular when each test uses a bounded number of displacements along contiguous line or attached paths, or along paths in a recorded tree of line steps, together with one common specified integer offset of size $O(\ell H)$.
  - Explanation: Reverse elimination multiplies prime-divisibility or invertible-congruence savings while preserving nonzero and coefficient tests.
- **claim-retained-line-predicate** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-retained-lines)): For each closed line there is a predicate $R_{\boldsymbol d}(n_{\mathcal F})\in\{0,1\}$ with the following properties. Whenever it equals one:* 1. *all core line occurrences are lit; no primitive specification with support contained in $\mathcal F$ qualifies at a trace vertex; and fewer than $t_0$ repeated center occurrences are unlit;* 2. *every fixed tree prime has $O(B^\rho)$ active components; fewer than $B^{1-2\rho}$ fixed tree primes have more than one active component;* 3. *fewer than $B^{1-2\rho}$ nonfixed tree primes are corrupted.* *The implicit constant is uniform in the line. Moreover, $$\label{eq:retained-line-error} \sum_{\boldsymbol d} \mathbb E\left[\|K_{\boldsymbol d}(n)\| \prod_{j=0}^{\ell}\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j) (1-R_{\boldsymbol d}(n_{\mathcal F}))\right] \ll \exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}).$$ The same exceptional estimates used in this proof remain valid after adding the qualification indicators of at most $2t_0$ specifications and summing their recorded data, for the classes discarded by an arithmetic saving. Configurations excluded because of a fixed forbidden specification instead vanish with the original $Y$ factors.
  - Explanation: A predicate on fixed residues removes exceptional activity patterns while leaving singleton center coordinates free.
- **claim-forest-path-hitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-forest-path-hitting)): Let a finite forest carry a finite family of nonempty edge paths. If the family has no $t$ pairwise edge-disjoint paths, some set of fewer than $2t$ edges meets every path in the family.
  - Explanation: Greedy deepest-top selection gives a small edge hitting set when a large edge-disjoint path family is absent.
- **claim-connected-activity-blocks** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-activity-blocks)): Except for configurations of zero contribution in <a href="#eq:retained-line-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retained-line-error">[eq:retained-line-error]</a> or total absolute contribution $O(\exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}))$, one can cut $O(t_0)$ edges of the first-visit tree and cover the resulting forest by $O(B^\rho)$ connected blocks of diameter at most $L$. The blocks cover every remaining edge and every vertex of the original tree. In each block, the active set of every fixed tree prime is connected or empty. Consequently each fixed tree prime has $O(B^\rho)$ whole-tree active components.
  - Explanation: After negligible or zero exclusions, cuts and traversal blocks make each fixed prime's activity connected within a block.
- **claim-two-block-rerooting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-reroot-constraints)): Let two connected blocks of a tree and $m$ distinct prime indices $p$ be given. For each index let $V_p$ be a vertex subset, and let $A_p^j$ be its intersection with block $j$. Suppose these intersections are nonempty and connected. Edges have labels containing at most $J$ prime factors. In either block, an edge has both endpoints in $A_p^j$ if and only if its label contains $p$. Assume also that $A_p^1$ and $A_p^2$ lie in distinct components of the subgraph induced by $V_p$ in the whole tree.* *There are roots $c_j$ in block $j$, a set of $\gg m^{1/8}/(2J+1)$ retained indices, and an order on those indices with the following property. If $x_p^j$ is the nearest vertex of $A_p^j$ to $c_j$, both paths from $c_j$ to $x_p^j$ avoid $p$, and any retained prime label on either path occurs earlier in the order. The two representatives $x_p^1,x_p^2$ are distinct.
  - Explanation: Ancestor-order selection and rerooting yield distinct active representatives with triangular prime dependencies.
- **claim-few-disconnected-fixed-primes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-few-disconnected)): Among the configurations retained by [lem:activity-blocks](#lem:activity-blocks), those with at least $B^{1-2\rho}$ fixed tree primes having multiple active components have total absolute contribution $O(\exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}))$.
  - Explanation: Many disconnected fixed labels give a common block pair and enough triangular divisibility tests for a negligible contribution.
- **claim-few-corrupted-singletons** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-few-corrupted)): Lines having at least $B^{1-2\rho}$ corrupted nonfixed tree primes have total absolute contribution $O(\exp(-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}))$.
  - Explanation: Many accidental offset coincidences for singleton tree primes yield independent or triangular numerical tests.
- **claim-finite-cylinder-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation)): Suppose that the cylinders in $\mathscr E$ have width at most $w\ge1$. For an integer $t\ge1$, put $M=tw$, and let $$\mathscr H_t=\{J\in\mathscr P:\operatorname{rk}(J)\ge t, \ J\text{ is generated by at most }t\text{ members of }\mathscr E\}.$$ Then the rank is nondecreasing on $\mathscr P$, every $I\in\mathscr P$ is generated by at most $\operatorname{rk}(I)$ events, and $$\label{eq:cylinder-width} \|\operatorname{supp}(I)\|\le w\operatorname{rk}(I).$$ For every $x\in\Omega$, $$\label{eq:cylinder-truncation} \left\|\mathbf 1_{x\notin\bigcup_{E\in\mathscr E}E} -\sum_{\operatorname{rk}(I)<t}\mu(\widehat0,I)\mathbf 1_I(x)\right\| \le 2^M\max(1,M^{M+1})\sum_{J\in\mathscr H_t}\mathbf 1_J(x).$$ Every coefficient retained in the sum on the left has absolute value at most $\max(1,M^{M+1})$. Every intersection on the right has width at most $M$, even if its rank is greater than $t$.
  - Explanation: Private-coordinate rank controls generating lists, support width and a finite Möbius truncation with an explicit pointwise error.
- **claim-private-cylinder-constraints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder-witnesses)): Let $\boldsymbol d$ be a numerical closed line, and let $\mathcal F$ contain its core prime labels and all labels occurring at least twice on the line. Suppose $t_0$ primitive specifications attached at line vertices qualify simultaneously. Suppose that each has a prime outside $\mathcal F$ absent from every other specification in this family. For sufficiently large $B$, their numerical labels satisfy a triangular system on at least $B^{2\varepsilon}$ distinct selected prime variables of the kind in [lem:triangular-saving](#lem:triangular-saving). Each constraint uses one intrinsic suffix displacement, or the difference of two witness prefixes and a line segment. Its nonzero expressions have logarithmic size $O(B\log B)$ even when numerical label-size restrictions are dropped.
  - Explanation: Jointly qualifying primitive witnesses with private free primes force a large triangular numerical constraint system.
- **claim-short-list-cylinder-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cylinder-reduction)): For the retained line and fixed-coordinate data of [prop:retained-lines](#prop:retained-lines), there is a factor $\mathfrak C_B=\exp(O(M_0\log B))$ such that $$\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\boldsymbol d}\mathbb E_{\mathcal F} \left[R_{\boldsymbol d} \left\|\mathbb E_{\mathcal S\setminus\mathcal F} K_{\boldsymbol d}(n)\prod_{j=0}^{\ell}\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\right\|\right] \notag\\ &\quad\le \mathfrak C_B\sum_{\boldsymbol d}\mathbb E_{\mathcal F} \left[R_{\boldsymbol d} \sum_{\mathfrak L\in\mathscr L_{<t_0}(\boldsymbol d)} \left\|\mathbb E_{\mathcal S\setminus\mathcal F} K_{\boldsymbol d}(n)I_{\mathfrak L}(n)\right\|\right] +O\!\left(e^{-B^{1+\varepsilon/2}}\right). \label{eq:cylinder-reduction} \end{aligned}$$ Here each sum runs over numerical closed lines, $\mathcal F$ is the fixed prime set of that line, and every list indicator imposes full qualification of its specifications.
  - Explanation: Conditioned avoidance is replaced by short qualification lists while signed expectations remain inside absolute values.
- **claim-first-visit-topology** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-topology)): For a closed line of length $\ell$ with $r_*$ returns, the topology of first visits and returns, together with all step signs, has at most $4^\ell(\ell+1)^{r_*}$ possibilities. Its tree satisfies $$\label{eq:trace-bad-edges} \|E(\mathscr T)\setminus\mathcal G\|\le 10(r_*+1),\qquad \ell=\|E(\mathscr T)\|+r_*.$$ Once the labels of the tree edges and this topology are specified, every return label is determined.
  - Explanation: First-visit and return topology has a controlled count, few nongood edges relative to returns, and no independent return-label choices.
- **claim-exact-subtree-weight** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-subtree-weight)): For every finite rooted tree and every $A_a>0$ with $\beta_a=(1+A_a)^{-1}$, $$\label{eq:subtree-identity} \sum_{Q:\,\|E(Q)\|\ge1}u_a(Q)=S_a(\mathscr T)\le \|V(\mathscr T)\|.$$
  - Explanation: A branching probability identity sums weighted nontrivial subtrees exactly, enabling cancellation of background prime factors.
- **claim-trace-token-record** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-token-record)): For retained data, these records have the following properties.* 1. *They recover every tree-edge label. Together with the topology and the list data they consequently recover the full line and list.* 2. *The total number of tagged tokens and list prime slots is at most $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$, for sufficiently large $B$.* 3. *An untagged fixed center prime cannot have a single good edge as its token.
  - Explanation: Tokens reconstruct the tree labels and lists, bound the exceptional record size and exclude a problematic fixed-center singleton shape.
- **claim-primewise-trace-integration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-integration)): Uniformly over retained lines, lists and assigned records, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal I(R) &\le \exp\left\{ o(1)-\kappa(v_C-T\sqrt{v_C})(r_*+\|\mathcal U\|)\right\} \notag\\ &\quad\times\exp\left\{ -v_CS_C(\mathscr T)-v_ZS_Z(\mathscr T) +v_C\beta_C(e^\kappa-1)\|\mathcal U\|\right\} \notag\\ &\quad\times\prod_{\substack{p\text{ distinct}\\ p\text{ in tree or list}}}\frac1p \prod_{\text{tagged tokens}}\mathrm{weight} \prod_{\substack{\text{untagged tokens}\\(a,Q)}}\widetilde u_a(Q). \label{eq:trace-integration} \end{aligned}$$ Primes occurring only on returns need not appear in the product over tree or list primes. The $o(1)$ tends to zero as $B$ tends to infinity, uniformly in the line, list and record, with $h,\tau,C_0,T$ and the constants in <a href="#eq:graph-constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-constants">[eq:graph-constants]</a> fixed.
  - Explanation: Free center residues are integrated with signs first; positive core and background bounds then supply cutoff penalties and token weights.
- **claim-trace-core-pivots** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-pivots)): Fix the topology, token shapes, tagged/list groupings and untagged multiplicities. For every non-skipped good origin $u\notin\mathcal U$ choose deterministically one named core slot whose token has top $u$. When summing prime assignments, the bin conditions on these edges give an extra factor $O(B^{-1+\eta})$ for each chosen slot, while retaining the unrestricted harmonic factor $v_a$ for every prime variable. Together with the charge exponent and its correction in <a href="#eq:trace-integration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trace-integration">[eq:trace-integration]</a>, these savings are bounded by $$\label{eq:trace-pivot-saving} B^{-100r_*}\exp(O(\ell)) B^{-(1-\eta)(\|\mathcal G\|-N_{\mathrm{exc}})}.$$
  - Explanation: Deterministic named core pivots allow reverse ancestor elimination of divisor-bin constraints without losing harmonic factors or factorial symmetry.
- **claim-conditional-trace-summation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-summation)): For sufficiently large $B$, the total conditional main term supplied by [prop:cylinder-reduction](#prop:cylinder-reduction), including its $\exp(O(M_0\log B))$ prefactor, is at most $B^{-(1+2c_*)\ell}$.
  - Explanation: Factorial token sums cancel the background exponential; pivot and center savings dominate topology and exceptional-record costs.
- **claim-character-conjugation-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-stability)): Let $f,G\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ be multiplicative, let $\psi$ be a fixed Dirichlet character, and let $S$ be a finite set of primes. If $G(p)=f(p)\psi(p)$ for every $p\notin S$, then for every Dirichlet character $\chi$, every real $t$, and every $X\ge2$, $$\left\|D(G,\chi n^{it};X)^2 -D(f,(\chi\overline\psi)n^{it};X)^2\right\| \le 2\sum_{p\in S}\frac1p. \label{eq:affine-stability}$$ Thus uniform nonpretentiousness of $f$ implies that of $G$. Complex conjugation also preserves uniform nonpretentiousness.
  - Explanation: Fixed character twists, finite prime changes and conjugation preserve uniform nonpretentiousness.
- **claim-finite-dilation-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-dilation)): Let $a\ge1$ be an integer and let $f\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ be multiplicative with $f(1)=1$. Define $$F_{a,f}(m)= \begin{cases} f(m/a),&a\mid m,\\ 0,&a\nmid m. \end{cases} \label{eq:affine-dilation-function}$$ Then $F_{a,f}$ is a signed sum of $2^{\omega(a)}$ normalized, $1$-bounded multiplicative functions, each agreeing with $f$ at every prime power whose prime does not divide $a$. If $f$ is uniformly nonpretentious, every function in this sum is uniformly nonpretentious.
  - Explanation: Local prime-power sequences produce a finite signed expansion of a dilated function into normalized bounded multiplicative functions.
- **claim-every-residue-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-residue)): Let $l\ge1$ and $0\le b<l$ be integers, and set $$d=\gcd(b,l),\qquad q=l/d,\qquad c=b/d.$$ For every positive integer $m$, $$\mathbf 1_{m\equiv b\pmod l} =\frac1{\varphi(q)}\sum_{\chi\bmod q} \overline{\chi(c)}F_{d,\chi}(m). \label{eq:affine-character-expansion}$$ Each $F_{d,\chi}$ is a signed sum of $2^{\omega(d)}$ normalized, $1$-bounded multiplicative functions agreeing with $\chi$ at all prime powers outside $d$. For $q=1$, the character in the formula is the constant function one, including at the residue $c=0$, and $\varphi(1)=1$.
  - Explanation: Extracting the common divisor of the residue and modulus permits character orthogonality and finite local expansion even for zero or nonunit classes.
- **claim-general-fixed-progression** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-affine-progression)): Let $f_1,f_2\colon\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ be multiplicative, with at least one uniformly nonpretentious. For fixed distinct nonnegative integers $h_1,h_2$, an integer $l\ge1$, and any integer $b$, $$\frac1X\sum_{1\le n\le X} f_1(n+h_1)f_2(n+h_2)\mathbf 1_{n\equiv b\pmod l} \longrightarrow0 \qquad(X\longrightarrow\infty). \label{eq:affine-progression-limit}$$ The limit holds through all real $X$, including nonunit residue classes.
  - Explanation: Finite residue expansions placed on an original nonpretentious factor give ordinary cancellation in every fixed residue class.
- **claim-liouville-uniform-nonpretentiousness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-liouville-unp)): For every fixed Dirichlet character $\chi$, $$\inf_{\|t\|\le X}D(\lambda,\chi n^{it};X) \ge \frac14\sqrt{\log\log X}-O_\chi(1) \qquad(X\ge100). \label{eq:affine-liouville-unp}$$ In particular, $\lambda$ is uniformly nonpretentious.
  - Explanation: The distance lower bound covers the entire growing twist-height interval, verifying the exact hypothesis required by the qualitative theorem.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-q-crude-count",
        "exact_quote": "Consider at most $R\\le4L$ signed steps together with additional records having at most $L^{O(1)}$ choices per prime slot. Then the number of equality patterns and such records is at most $\\exp(O(R\\log^2L))$. Its reciprocal sum costs at most $(1+L)/H_0$ in place of an unrestricted prime sum. Such savings multiply when selected variables are ordered so that each relation uses only its own variable, earlier selected variables, and nonselected variables.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $m\\ge1$, a bin $j$, tuples $d_1,\\ldots,d_m\\in\\mathcal D$, signs $\\varepsilon_1,\\ldots,\\varepsilon_m\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, an initial site, and the entire non-$P$ residue environment.         "anchor": "stmt-q-noncommuting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$ and let $B_1,\\ldots,B_N$ be self-adjoint operators on a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$, and let $P$ be an orthogonal projection on $\\mathcal H$.         "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-007.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-raw-lower-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,f_2\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb C$ be $1$-bounded multiplicative functions, with $f_1(1)=f_2(1)=1$, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:analytic-bias\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-bias\">[eq:analytic-bias]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:analytic-finite-defects\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-finite-defects\">[eq:analytic-finite-defects]</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-analytic-centering",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,f_2\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb C$ be multiplicative, with $|f_i|\\le1$, and suppose at least one is uniformly nonpretentious. Fix $h\\ge1$, $1<\\tau<2$, $C_0\\ge1$, $T>0$, and a smooth function $0\\le\\phi\\le1$ supported in $[-T,T]$. For each sufficiently large $B$, let $\\mathcal D,H,J$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:divisor-family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:divisor-family\">[eq:divisor-family]</a>, let $|a_d|\\le1$, and use <a href=\"#eq:analytic-cutoffs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-cutoffs\">[eq:analytic-cutoffs]</a>. The long-variable limit is taken with $B$ fixed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-prime-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f,b\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb C$ be multiplicative and $1$-bounded. Suppose their prime values agree outside a finite set $\\mathcal P$. For every Dirichlet character $\\chi$, every real $t$, and $X\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:analytic-distance-stability}\n \\left|D(b,\\chi n^{it};X)^2-D(f,\\chi n^{it};X)^2\\right|\n \\le2\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\frac1p.$$ Consequently, if $f$ is uniformly nonpretentious, the same divergence holds uniformly over all such $b$ and over every fixed finite family of Dirichlet characters.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-mrt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X\\ge D\\ge10$ and let $b\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb C$ be a $1$-bounded multiplicative function. With $Q=\\min((\\log X)^{1/125},(\\log D)^5)$, one has $$\\label{eq:mrt-input}\n \\begin{split}\n \\sup_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\int_0^X\n \\left|\\sum_{y<m\\le y+D}b(m)\\mathrm e(\\alpha m)\\right|\\,dy\n \\ll DX\\left(\n e^{-\\mathcal M(b;X,Q)/20}\n +\\frac{\\log\\log D}{\\log D}\n +\\frac1{(\\log X)^{1/700}}\\right).\n \\end{split}$$ The implied constant is absolute.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-mrt",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X\\ge D\\ge10$ and let $b\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb C$ be a $1$-bounded multiplicative function.           "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-multiplier",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $1<\\tau<2$, and an integer $h\\ge1$. Let $P_0=\\exp(B^{1-\\varepsilon})$, where $\\varepsilon=10^{-4}$, and suppose $P_0/\\tau\\le M\\le\\exp(C_0B)$. If $|c_w|\\le1$ and $c_w=0$ unless $w\\in(M,\\tau M]$ is $P_0$-rough, then, for all sufficiently large $B$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|Q_M\\|_\\infty&\\ll B^{-1+\\varepsilon}\\log B,\n \\label{eq:rough-sup}\\\\\n \\int_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}|Q_M(\\alpha)|^4\\,d\\alpha\n &\\ll M^{-1}B^{-4+4\\varepsilon}(\\log B)^6.\n \\label{eq:rough-fourth}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in $M$ and the coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-multiplier",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $1<\\tau<2$, and an integer $h\\ge1$. Let $P_0=\\exp(B^{1-\\varepsilon})$, where $\\varepsilon=10^{-4}$, and suppose $P_0/\\tau\\le M\\le\\exp(C_0B)$.           "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $1<\\tau<2$, and an integer $h\\ge1$. Let $P_0=\\exp(B^{1-\\varepsilon})$, where $\\varepsilon=10^{-4}$, and suppose $P_0/\\tau\\le M\\le\\exp(C_0B)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-primitive-properties",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $B$, let a primitive specification have edge labels $e_1,\\ldots,e_m$, extra prime $p$, and suffix ending at $m$. Then the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *For every edge prime $q$, the indices $i$ with $q\\mid e_i$ form an interval of consecutive indices.*\n\n2.  *Let $b$ be the first index of the final maximal constant block $e_b=\\cdots=e_m$. Then $b>1$, the signs on this block are constant, and there exists $r\\mid e_b$ with $r\\nmid e_{b-1}$. Every such $r$ occurs only in the constant tail. In the specified suffix displacement, its coefficient as a prime variable is nonzero modulo $p$. This coefficient assertion holds for any edge prime whose occurrences are confined to that tail.*\n\n3.  *If $q$ occurs before $b$ and $r$ is as in the preceding part, the coefficient of $q$ in $z_{b-1}-z_0$ is nonzero modulo $r$.*\n\n*Coefficients here are obtained by fixing all other distinct prime values; squarefreeness makes each displacement linear in the prime being varied.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },

Then $b>1$, the signs on this block are constant, and there exists $r\\mid e_b$ with $r\\nmid e_{b-1}$. Every such $r$ occurs only in the constant tail. In the specified suffix displacement, its coefficient as a prime variable is nonzero modulo $p$. This coefficient assertion holds for any edge prime whose occurrences are confined to that tail.*\n\n3.  *If $q$ occurs before $b$ and $r$ is as in the preceding part, the coefficient of $q$ in $z_{b-1}-z_0$ is nonzero modulo $r$.*\n\n*Coefficients here are obtained by fixing all other distinct prime values; squarefreeness makes each displacement linear in the prime being varied.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-crude-count",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a line of length $\\ell$ together with at most $2t_0$ attached qualifying specifications, each of length at most $L$. Refine its slot pattern by recording the lit or unlit status of every line occurrence. There are $\\exp(O(B\\log^2B))$ slot patterns, including signs, attachments, occurrence statuses, and recorded vertex identifications. For each assigned pattern and its numerical prime values, let $\\mathfrak A$ be its attached specifications and let $E$ be any residue event imposing the recorded occurrence statuses, possibly with further restrictions. Then $$\\label{eq:absolute-pattern-majorant}\n \\mathbb E\\left[|K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)|\\mathbf 1_E(n)\n     \\prod_{\\sigma\\in\\mathfrak A}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\sigma\\text{ qualifies at its assigned vertex}\\}}\\right]\n \\le A^{\\ell J}\\prod_{p\\text{ used}}\\frac1p.$$ If the recorded statuses include at least $t_0$ unlit occurrences among center primes occurring at least twice on the line, this majorant has the additional factor $P_0^{-t_0/2}$. The total contribution of that class, summed over all patterns and numerical assignments, is $O(\\exp(-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}))$.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-crude-count",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a line of length $\\ell$ together with at most $2t_0$ attached qualifying specifications, each of length at most $L$. There are $\\exp(O(B\\log^2B))$ slot patterns, including signs, attachments, occurrence statuses, and recorded vertex identifications. For each assigned pattern and its numerical prime values, let $\\mathfrak A$ be its attached specifications and let $E$ be any residue event imposing the recorded occurrence statuses, possibly with further restrictions. Then $$\\label{eq:absolute-pattern-majorant}\n \\mathbb E\\left[|K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)|\\mathbf 1_E(n)\n     \\prod_{\\sigma\\in\\mathfrak A}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\sigma\\text{ qualifies at its assigned vertex}\\}}\\right]\n \\le A^{\\ell J}\\prod_{p\\text{ used}}\\frac1p.$$ If the recorded statuses include at least $t_0$ unlit occurrences among center primes occurring at least twice on the line, this majorant has the additional factor $P_0^{-t_0/2}$. The total contribution of that class, summed over all patterns and numerical assignments, is $O(\\exp(-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}))$.",
          "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-triangular-saving",
        "exact_quote": "In the positive count of [lem:crude-count](#lem:crude-count), suppose every configuration under consideration supplies at least $B^{2\\varepsilon}$ distinct prime variables $q_1,\\ldots,q_k$ and tests with the following properties. Test $j$ uses only $q_j$, the earlier variables $q_1,\\ldots,q_{j-1}$, and nonselected variables, and is either*\n\n1.  *$q_j\\mid D_j$, where $D_j\\ne0$ is independent of $q_j$ and $\\log(|D_j|+2)=O(B\\log B)$; or*\n\n2.  *$a_jq_j+c_j\\equiv0\\pmod{r_j}$, where $a_j,c_j,r_j$ are independent of $q_j$, $r_j$ is an earlier or nonselected prime variable, and $a_j\\not\\equiv0\\pmod{r_j}$.*\n\n\n*Assume the choices of tests and their order have $\\exp(O(B\\log^2B))$ descriptions. Then their total absolute contribution, also allowing the attached specifications in [lem:crude-count](#lem:crude-count), is $$\\label{eq:negligible-constraints}\n O\\bigl(\\exp(-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2})\\bigr).$$ The description bound applies in particular when each test uses a bounded number of displacements along contiguous line or attached paths, or along paths in a recorded tree of line steps, together with one common specified integer offset of size $O(\\ell H)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-triangular-saving",

Then their total absolute contribution, also allowing the attached specifications in [lem:crude-count](#lem:crude-count), is $$\\label{eq:negligible-constraints}\n O\\bigl(\\exp(-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2})\\bigr).$$ The description bound applies in particular when each test uses a bounded number of displacements along contiguous line or attached paths, or along paths in a recorded tree of line steps, together with one common specified integer offset of size $O(\\ell H)$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-retained-lines",
        "exact_quote": "For each closed line there is a predicate $R_{\\boldsymbol d}(n_{\\mathcal F})\\in\\{0,1\\}$ with the following properties. Whenever it equals one:*\n\n1.  *all core line occurrences are lit; no primitive specification with support contained in $\\mathcal F$ qualifies at a trace vertex; and fewer than $t_0$ repeated center occurrences are unlit;*\n\n2.  *every fixed tree prime has $O(B^\\rho)$ active components; fewer than $B^{1-2\\rho}$ fixed tree primes have more than one active component;*\n\n3.  *fewer than $B^{1-2\\rho}$ nonfixed tree primes are corrupted.*\n\n\n*The implicit constant is uniform in the line. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:retained-line-error}\n \\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\n  \\mathbb E\\left[|K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)|\n       \\prod_{j=0}^{\\ell}\\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\n       (1-R_{\\boldsymbol d}(n_{\\mathcal F}))\\right]\n \\ll \\exp(-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}).$$ The same exceptional estimates used in this proof remain valid after adding the qualification indicators of at most $2t_0$ specifications and summing their recorded data, for the classes discarded by an arithmetic saving. Configurations excluded because of a fixed forbidden specification instead vanish with the original $Y$ factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reroot-constraints",
        "exact_quote": "Let two connected blocks of a tree and $m$ distinct prime indices $p$ be given. For each index let $V_p$ be a vertex subset, and let $A_p^j$ be its intersection with block $j$. Suppose these intersections are nonempty and connected. Edges have labels containing at most $J$ prime factors. In either block, an edge has both endpoints in $A_p^j$ if and only if its label contains $p$. Assume also that $A_p^1$ and $A_p^2$ lie in distinct components of the subgraph induced by $V_p$ in the whole tree.*\n\n*There are roots $c_j$ in block $j$, a set of $\\gg m^{1/8}/(2J+1)$ retained indices, and an order on those indices with the following property. If $x_p^j$ is the nearest vertex of $A_p^j$ to $c_j$, both paths from $c_j$ to $x_p^j$ avoid $p$, and any retained prime label on either path occurs earlier in the order. The two representatives $x_p^1,x_p^2$ are distinct.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the cylinders in $\\mathscr E$ have width at most $w\\ge1$. For an integer $t\\ge1$, put $M=tw$, and let $$\\mathscr H_t=\\{J\\in\\mathscr P:\\operatorname{rk}(J)\\ge t,\n       \\ J\\text{ is generated by at most }t\\text{ members of }\\mathscr E\\}.$$ Then the rank is nondecreasing on $\\mathscr P$, every $I\\in\\mathscr P$ is generated by at most $\\operatorname{rk}(I)$ events, and $$\\label{eq:cylinder-width}\n |\\operatorname{supp}(I)|\\le w\\operatorname{rk}(I).$$ For every $x\\in\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:cylinder-truncation}\n \\left|\\mathbf 1_{x\\notin\\bigcup_{E\\in\\mathscr E}E}\n       -\\sum_{\\operatorname{rk}(I)<t}\\mu(\\widehat0,I)\\mathbf 1_I(x)\\right|\n \\le 2^M\\max(1,M^{M+1})\\sum_{J\\in\\mathscr H_t}\\mathbf 1_J(x).$$ Every coefficient retained in the sum on the left has absolute value at most $\\max(1,M^{M+1})$. Every intersection on the right has width at most $M$, even if its rank is greater than $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation",

        {

          "exact_quote": "Then the rank is nondecreasing on $\\mathscr P$, every $I\\in\\mathscr P$ is generated by at most $\\operatorname{rk}(I)$ events,",
          "source_id": "PM-007.1"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation",
          "exact_quote": "Every coefficient retained in the sum on the left has absolute value at most $\\max(1,M^{M+1})$. Every intersection on the right has width at most $M$, even if its rank is greater than $t$.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cylinder-truncation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* If $I\\le J$, every event containing $I$ also contains $J$. Thus every family admitted for the maximum defining $\\operatorname{rk}(I)$ is admitted for $\\operatorname{rk}(J)$, proving monotonicity. Choose an inclusion-minimal family generating $I$. Each member must have a private coordinate: otherwise all its prescribed values already follow from the other members, since the prescriptions are compatible, and it can be removed. Its size is therefore at most $\\operatorname{rk}(I)$, proving <a href=\"#eq:cylinder-width\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cylinder-width\">[eq:cylinder-width]</a> as well.\n\n\nFix $J\\in\\mathscr P$ and let $m=|\\operatorname{supp}(J)|$. An element of the interval $[\\widehat0,J]$ is determined by its support: its values must be the corresponding restrictions of those prescribed by $J$. Consequently this interval has at most $2^m$ elements. Along a strict chain from $\\widehat0$ to $J$, supports grow strictly, so there are at most $m$ steps. A chain of $k$ steps is specified by recording, for each coordinate of $\\operatorname{supp}(J)$, the step at which it enters. There are at most $k^m$ such chains. Expanding the inverse of the poset’s upper triangular incidence matrix, or applying the Möbius recursion repeatedly, gives the alternating sum over these chains. Thus, for $m\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:cylinder-mobius}\n |\\mu(\\widehat0,J)|\\le\\sum_{k=1}^m k^m\\le m^{m+1};$$ the coefficient at $\\widehat0$ is $1$. This proves the asserted coefficient bound for intersections of rank below $t$.",

        },

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-witnesses",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\boldsymbol d$ be a numerical closed line, and let $\\mathcal F$ contain its core prime labels and all labels occurring at least twice on the line. Suppose $t_0$ primitive specifications attached at line vertices qualify simultaneously. Suppose that each has a prime outside $\\mathcal F$ absent from every other specification in this family. For sufficiently large $B$, their numerical labels satisfy a triangular system on at least $B^{2\\varepsilon}$ distinct selected prime variables of the kind in [lem:triangular-saving](#lem:triangular-saving). Each constraint uses one intrinsic suffix displacement, or the difference of two witness prefixes and a line segment. Its nonzero expressions have logarithmic size $O(B\\log B)$ even when numerical label-size restrictions are dropped.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cylinder-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "For the retained line and fixed-coordinate data of [prop:retained-lines](#prop:retained-lines), there is a factor $\\mathfrak C_B=\\exp(O(M_0\\log B))$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n          K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)\\prod_{j=0}^{\\ell}\\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\\right|\\right]\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad\\le \\mathfrak C_B\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\sum_{\\mathfrak L\\in\\mathscr L_{<t_0}(\\boldsymbol d)}\n       \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n                      K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)I_{\\mathfrak L}(n)\\right|\\right]\n       +O\\!\\left(e^{-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:cylinder-reduction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here each sum runs over numerical closed lines, $\\mathcal F$ is the fixed prime set of that line, and every list indicator imposes full qualification of its specifications.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cylinder-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "For the retained line and fixed-coordinate data of [prop:retained-lines](#prop:retained-lines), there is a factor $\\mathfrak C_B=\\exp(O(M_0\\log B))$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n          K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)\\prod_{j=0}^{\\ell}\\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\\right|\\right]\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad\\le \\mathfrak C_B\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\sum_{\\mathfrak L\\in\\mathscr L_{<t_0}(\\boldsymbol d)}\n       \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n                      K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)I_{\\mathfrak L}(n)\\right|\\right]\n       +O\\!\\left(e^{-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:cylinder-reduction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here each sum runs over numerical closed lines, $\\mathcal F$ is the fixed prime set of that line, and every list indicator imposes full qualification of its specifications.",
          "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the retained line and fixed-coordinate data of [prop:retained-lines](#prop:retained-lines), there is a factor $\\mathfrak C_B=\\exp(O(M_0\\log B))$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n          K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)\\prod_{j=0}^{\\ell}\\mathbf 1_Y(n+b_j)\\right|\\right]\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad\\le \\mathfrak C_B\\sum_{\\boldsymbol d}\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal F}\n   \\left[R_{\\boldsymbol d}\n     \\sum_{\\mathfrak L\\in\\mathscr L_{<t_0}(\\boldsymbol d)}\n       \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\mathcal S\\setminus\\mathcal F}\n                      K_{\\boldsymbol d}(n)I_{\\mathfrak L}(n)\\right|\\right]\n       +O\\!\\left(e^{-B^{1+\\varepsilon/2}}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:cylinder-reduction}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here each sum runs over numerical closed lines, $\\mathcal F$ is the fixed prime set of that line, and every list indicator imposes full qualification of its specifications.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-topology",
        "exact_quote": "For a closed line of length $\\ell$ with $r_*$ returns, the topology of first visits and returns, together with all step signs, has at most $4^\\ell(\\ell+1)^{r_*}$ possibilities. Its tree satisfies $$\\label{eq:trace-bad-edges}\n |E(\\mathscr T)\\setminus\\mathcal G|\\le 10(r_*+1),\\qquad\n \\ell=|E(\\mathscr T)|+r_*.$$ Once the labels of the tree edges and this topology are specified, every return label is determined.",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-topology",
          "exact_quote": "For a closed line of length $\\ell$ with $r_*$ returns, the topology of first visits and returns, together with all step signs, has at most $4^\\ell(\\ell+1)^{r_*}$ possibilities. Its tree satisfies $$\\label{eq:trace-bad-edges}\n |E(\\mathscr T)\\setminus\\mathcal G|\\le 10(r_*+1),\\qquad\n \\ell=|E(\\mathscr T)|+r_*.$$ Once the labels of the tree edges and this topology are specified, every return label is determined.",
          "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a closed line of length $\\ell$ with $r_*$ returns, the topology of first visits and returns, together with all step signs, has at most $4^\\ell(\\ell+1)^{r_*}$ possibilities.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-pivots",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the topology, token shapes, tagged/list groupings and untagged multiplicities. For every non-skipped good origin $u\\notin\\mathcal U$ choose deterministically one named core slot whose token has top $u$. When summing prime assignments, the bin conditions on these edges give an extra factor $O(B^{-1+\\eta})$ for each chosen slot, while retaining the unrestricted harmonic factor $v_a$ for every prime variable. Together with the charge exponent and its correction in <a href=\"#eq:trace-integration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:trace-integration\">[eq:trace-integration]</a>, these savings are bounded by $$\\label{eq:trace-pivot-saving}\n B^{-100r_*}\\exp(O(\\ell))\n       B^{-(1-\\eta)(|\\mathcal G|-N_{\\mathrm{exc}})}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-007.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,f_2\\colon\\mathbb N\\to\\mathbb D$ be multiplicative, with at least one uniformly nonpretentious.           "source_id": "PM-007.1"
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Liouville function and prime-factor count** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $n\ge1$, let $\Omega(n)$ be the number of prime factors of $n$, counted with multiplicity, and let $\lambda(n)=(-1)^{\Omega(n)}$.
- **Bounded multiplicative functions** ([source](#eq:distance)): Let $\mathbb D=\{z\in\mathbb C:\|z\|\le1\}$. A function $f:\mathbb N\to\mathbb D$ is multiplicative when $f(mn)=f(m)f(n)$ for coprime positive integers $m,n$.
- **Pretentious distance** ([source](#eq:distance)): For a Dirichlet character $\chi$, a real number $t$, and $X\ge2$, define the nonnegative distance $D$ by $$\label{eq:distance} D(f,\chi n^{it};X)^2 =\sum_{p\le X}\frac{1-\operatorname{Re} (f(p)\overline{\chi(p)p^{it}})}p.$$
- **Uniform nonpretentiousness** ([source](#eq:unp)): We call $f$ *uniformly nonpretentious* if, for every fixed Dirichlet character, $$\label{eq:unp} \inf_{\|t\|\le N}D(f,\chi n^{it};N)\longrightarrow\infty \qquad(N\longrightarrow\infty).$$
- **Independent translated residue law** ([source](#pm-h-11)): To use this cancellation simultaneously at many primes, we introduce the auxiliary product law with one independent uniform residue modulo each $p\in P\cup Q$, and one independent uniform residue modulo $l$.
- **Active padding-prime set** ([source](#q:q-state-count)): For an offset $a$, its *active $Q$-set* is $$Q_a=\{p\in Q:R_p+a\equiv0\pmod p\}.$$
- **Forward prohibited word, minimal word and witness** ([source](#stmt-q-prohibited)): A numerical word is *forward prohibited* if it has length $3\le m\le s$ and satisfies three conditions: 1. Consecutive whole tuples $d_i,d_{i+1}$ are unequal. 2. For every prime in $P$, its indices of appearance among $d_1,\ldots,d_m$ form an interval, if nonempty. 3. Some prime $p\mid d_1$, absent from $d_m$, satisfies $$\label{q:prohibited-relation} p\mid\sum_{i=a}^m D_i\qquad\text{for some }1<a<m.$$ A forward prohibited word is *minimal* if no shorter contiguous subword is forward prohibited in either orientation. A *witness* is a positive path realizing a minimal forward prohibited word. Let $\mathcal B_j$ be the set of sites at which a witness starts, and set $\sigma_j(n)=\mathbf 1_{n\notin\mathcal B_j}$.
- **Active label of a minimal prohibited word** ([source](#q:active-definition)): For a minimal prohibited word with steps $E_1,\ldots,E_m$, call a $P$ label $x$ *active* if there are a $P$ label $c$ of the same word and an interval $I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}$ such that $$\label{q:active-definition} c\mid\sum_{a\in I}E_a, \qquad \sum_{\substack{a\in I\\x\mid E_a}}E_a\not\equiv0\pmod c.$$
- **Closed line and normalized kernel** ([source](#eq:trace-parameters)): A *closed line* is a list of signs and labels $\boldsymbol d=((\varepsilon_i,d_i))_{i=1}^{\ell}$, where $\varepsilon_i\in\{-1,1\}$, $d_i\in\mathcal D$, and the offsets $$b_0=0,\qquad b_j=\sum_{i=1}^j\varepsilon_i h d_i \quad(1\le j\le\ell)$$ satisfy $b_\ell=0$.
- **Path specification, primitive specification and retained vertex set** ([source](#stmt-def-primitive-specification)): A specification consists of a path with distinct integer offsets $z_0=0,z_1,\ldots,z_m$, where $1\le m\le L$ and $$z_i-z_{i-1}=\varepsilon_i h e_i, \qquad e_i\in\mathcal D,\quad \varepsilon_i\in\{-1,1\},$$ an extra prime $p\in\mathcal S$ dividing none of the $e_i$, and an index $i_0\in\{1,\ldots,m\}$ such that $p\mid z_m-z_{i_0-1}$. It *qualifies at $x$* when $$p\mid x,\qquad e_i\mid x+z_{i-1}\quad(1\le i\le m).$$ Its prime support $\Pi$ is the union of $\{p\}$ and the prime factors of all its edge labels. Its *cylinder* is the set of starting integers $x$ satisfying these qualification conditions. The cylinder is either empty or fixes one residue at each prime in $\Pi$; in the latter case its measure is $\prod_{q\in\Pi}q^{-1}$. When testing a contiguous subpath as a specification, translate its offsets to start at zero. If it starts at $z_a$, test qualification at $x+z_a$. For the reversed subpath, use the same convention with its new initial vertex. The extra prime may be any member of $\Pi$ absent from the subpath’s edge labels. All these tests are constant as $x$ ranges over a nonempty original cylinder, since they use only its fixed prime coordinates. A specification is *primitive* if its cylinder is nonempty and no shorter nonempty contiguous subpath, in either orientation, can be made into a specification, with an extra prime from $\Pi$, that qualifies at its own initial vertex for $x$ in that cylinder. Set $$Y=\{x\in\mathbb Z:\text{no primitive specification qualifies at }x\}.$$
- **First-visit tree and return** ([source](#pm-h-56)): For a closed line $\boldsymbol d$, construct a rooted tree $\mathscr T$ on its distinct offsets as follows. Start at $b_0=0$. Whenever a step reaches an offset not visited previously, add that vertex and join it to the current vertex by the step just taken.
- **Exact cylinder, support and width** ([source](#pm-h-61)): An *exact cylinder* specifies one value at each coordinate in a subset of $P$ and imposes no condition at the other coordinates. Its *support* is the set of specified coordinates, and its *width* is the size of that support.
- **Private-coordinate intersection rank** ([source](#eq:cylinder-rank)): For $I\in\mathscr P$, define $$\label{eq:cylinder-rank} \operatorname{rk}(I)= \max\left\{\|\mathscr A\|:\begin{array}{l} \mathscr A\subseteq\{E\in\mathscr E:I\subseteq E\},\\ \text{each }E\in\mathscr A\text{ has a support coordinate}\\ \text{absent from every other member of }\mathscr A \end{array}\right\}.$$
- **Dilation function** ([source](#stmt-lem-affine-dilation)): Define $$F_{a,f}(m)= \begin{cases} f(m/a),&a\mid m,\\ 0,&a\nmid m. \end{cases} \label{eq:affine-dilation-function}$$

```json
          "source_id": "PM-007.1"
        }
      ],

      "interpretation": "The function evaluates the original factor on the quotient when the dilation divides the argument and vanishes otherwise.",
      "kind": "definition",

}
```
